# Manzil — Implementation Notes

| | |
|---|---|
| **Version** | 2.0.171 |
| **Status** | Living — churns freely, no ceremony required. **v2.0 is the implementation-start baseline**: further changes should come from code reality, not further pre-code polishing |
| **Sibling** | `DESIGN.md` (intent + contracts; wins all conflicts about *what* and *why*) |
| **Repo location** | `/IMPLEMENTATION.md` |

**Division of authority:** DESIGN.md owns intent, requirements, and cross-component contracts. This document owns *current mechanics* — how things are actually built right now. Code and docstrings win on exact interfaces; this doc points at modules rather than duplicating signatures once they exist. If this doc and DESIGN.md disagree, stop and flag it (CLAUDE.md rule) — do not silently pick a side. Update protocol here is deliberately lightweight: edit in place, add a line to the [Changelog](#9-changelog). No decision-log ceremony; that lives in DESIGN.md §20 for *design* changes only.

`docs/scoped-criteria-and-amenities.md` is the supplementary decision workbook for the P3-SC series. DESIGN (currently v3.92) and this document supersede it wherever wording or mechanics differ. `docs/onnx-image-classification.md` is the operating guide for the authoritative local ONNX `IMAGE_CLASSIFY` backend.

**Status labels.** Every section below carries one, so nobody — human or agent — has to guess how binding a given detail is:

- **Settled** — follow as written. Changing it takes a changelog entry (and a DESIGN §20 entry if it touches intent).
- **Proposal** — seeds the first implementation, then *follows the code*. Divergence here is expected and is not a doc/code conflict; update the doc to match reality without ceremony.
- **Interim / Conditional** — deliberately temporary or outcome-dependent, with the removal point or gating condition named inline.

## 1. Environment and Setup

*Status: settled.*

### Prerequisites
`uv` ≥ 0.5 · Node 20 + `pnpm` · Supabase CLI · Playwright (`uv run playwright install chromium` after worker sync).

### Bootstrap (once)
```bash
uv init --bare                              # root pyproject
# add [tool.uv.workspace] members = ["shared","api","worker"] to root pyproject.toml
uv init --lib shared && uv init --app api && uv init --app worker
# in api/ and worker/ pyproject: dependencies += ["manzil-shared"]
#   [tool.uv.sources] manzil-shared = { workspace = true }
uv sync --all-packages   # plain `uv sync` installs only the root package's deps

pnpm create vite frontend --template react-ts
supabase init && supabase start             # local stack
ln -s CLAUDE.md AGENTS.md
```

### Environment variables (`infra/.env.example`)

| Var | Used by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` | worker | sole LLM gateway (OpenRouter); replaces direct Anthropic/Google keys |
| `OPENROUTER_HTTP_REFERER` / `OPENROUTER_APP_TITLE` | worker | optional OpenRouter app attribution headers; referer falls back to `MANZIL_FRONTEND_URL`; title defaults to `Manzil` |
| `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` / `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY` / `LANGFUSE_HOST` | worker | cloud free tier; wired before first LLM call (NFR6). The API makes no LLM calls and gets no Langfuse keys |
| `DATABASE_URL` | worker, api | direct Postgres (worker uses service-level access) |
| `SUPABASE_URL` / `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | frontend, api | anon key is RLS-safe by design |
| `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` | worker, api | opaque `sb_secret_...` in hosted environments; the local CLI's legacy service-role JWT may occupy this variable for local-only compatibility. Worker always; API also needs it for narrowly privileged operations while hosting the in-process worker — never in frontend env |
| `MANZIL_WORKER_INPROCESS` | api | `true` by default (DESIGN §5); set to `false` only after a separate worker process is deployed and validated per §8 |
| `MANZIL_LOG_PROBE_REQUESTS` | api | unset by default: successful `/v1/health` and `/v1/ready` access lines are filtered out of `uvicorn.access` (failing ones always log). `true` restores every line (DESIGN §20 v3.79) |
| `MANZIL_IMAGE_CLASSIFY_ONNX_DIR` | worker, api in-process loop | required canonical IMAGE_CLASSIFY artifact directory containing pinned `model.onnx` + `manifest.json`; deployed package needs the `vision-onnx` extra. Missing configuration fails a non-empty IMAGE_CLASSIFY Stage; there is no LLM fallback. The former `MANZIL_IMAGE_CLASSIFY_ONNX_SHADOW_DIR` is accepted temporarily as a path alias |
| `API_CORS_ORIGINS` | api | frontend dev origin(s), comma-separated |
| `API_ENVIRONMENT` | api | `local` \| `staging` \| `production` — gates OpenAPI docs exposure |
| `MANZIL_EMAIL_MODE` | api | `disabled` (local/staging opt-out) \| `resend`; production startup requires `resend` so mandatory Hunt invitations cannot silently become no-ops |
| `RESEND_API_KEY` / `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | api | server-only product-mail send key and signing secret. Separate from the Resend SMTP credential held by Supabase Auth |
| `MANZIL_MAIL_FROM` / `MANZIL_MAIL_FROM_NAME` | api | verified product-mail sender identity; name defaults to `Manzil` |
| `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` / `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | frontend | public, RLS-safe by design |
| `VITE_API_BASE_URL` | frontend | e.g. `http://localhost:8000` locally |
| `VITE_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` | frontend | Maps JavaScript API **browser** key for the §13.2 map surfaces (DESIGN §20 2026-07-26). **Separate key from `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`**, restricted by HTTP referrer — every `VITE_*` value ships to the visitor, so the worker's server key must never be reused. Unset = map surfaces render an explanatory placeholder; nothing else breaks |
| `MANZIL_TIER3_PROVIDER` | worker | `brightdata` (default) \| `scrapingbee` — tier-3 unblocker adapter (§10.7, free plans only) |
| `BRIGHTDATA_API_KEY` / `BRIGHTDATA_ZONE` | worker | Bright Data Web Unlocker. **Both required, no default** — `BRIGHTDATA_ZONE` is the *zone* name from the control panel, **not** the API key's name (`BRIGHTDATA_KEY_NAME` is an accepted alias for the same value; a blank value counts as unset). Either one missing = tier 3 off the ladder, with a `tier3_partially_configured` warning (DESIGN §20 v3.78) |
| `SCRAPINGBEE_API_KEY` | worker | only when `MANZIL_TIER3_PROVIDER=scrapingbee` |
| `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` | worker | Phase 3 |
| `MANZIL_MODE` | worker | `workflow` (default) \| `agents` |
| `MANZIL_LLM_MODE` | worker, tests | `live` \| `record` \| `replay` (see §5) |
| `MANZIL_MODEL_<STAGE>` | worker, bench | per-stage model-pin override for bench/dev runs (e.g. `MANZIL_MODEL_EXTRACT=google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite`); must be priced in `llm/config.py` |

### Daily loop
```bash
supabase db reset                            # migrations + generated seed
uv run --package worker manzil ingest <url>  # Phase 0 entry point
#   (`manzil` is a [project.scripts] entry in worker/pyproject.toml → manzil_worker.cli:app)
uv run --package shared pytest               # engine goldens
uv run --package worker pytest               # stages vs fixtures (replay mode)
uv run ruff check --fix . && uv run ruff format .
```

---

## 2. Coding Conventions

*Status: settled — except the exception taxonomy below, which is a proposal (its names and granularity will follow what the runner actually needs).*

**Python.** 3.12, `ruff` for lint+format (line length 100), full type hints — `mypy --strict` on `shared/` (the engine must be airtight), standard on the rest. Worker is async end-to-end; no blocking I/O outside `asyncio.to_thread`. Logging via `structlog`, never `print`; every log line in the worker carries `job_id` and `stage`.

**Import direction (enforced by convention now, `ruff` isort sections + a lint script later):** `shared` imports nothing from `api`/`worker`. `api` and `worker` import `shared`. `worker/.../agents/` imports the truth layer and runner contracts; **nothing outside `agents/` imports from `agents/`**. Only `worker/.../llm/` imports provider SDKs.

**Exception taxonomy** (in `shared/errors.py` — stages raise these, the runner maps them to job outcomes):
`StageRetryable` (backoff + retry) · `StageFatal` (job → failed) · `FetchBlocked` / `FetchShell` (tier escalation signals) · `ExtractionInvalid` (one corrective retry, then fatal) · `AgentBudgetExceeded` (agents mode; stage decides fallback) · `CheckpointRaised(prompt)` (runner persists → `waiting_user`).

**Database access (the gap a developer hits in week one of Phase 1):** no ORM. The **worker** connects directly via `asyncpg` with the service role — plain SQL, Pydantic mapping at the boundary, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and the scheduler queries are hand-written and tested. The **API** performs user-context mutations through `supabase-py` with the caller's access token, so PostgREST evaluates RLS with the real JWT — the API never simulates permissions it can hand to the database. The narrow exception: API operations that legitimately need the service role (accepting an invite writes `hunt_members` before membership exists) go through an explicitly named `privileged.py` module — small on purpose, so privileged surface is greppable. **Frontend** reads table data straight from Supabase (RLS-guarded selects, per DESIGN §5.1) and mutates only via the API.

**CI (GitHub Actions):** four jobs on every PR — `ruff check` + `ruff format --check`; `mypy --strict` on `shared/`; `pytest` for all packages with `MANZIL_LLM_MODE=replay` (a replay miss fails the build — CI cannot spend tokens); frontend `eslint` + `vitest` from Phase 1. Branching: trunk-based, short-lived branches named by task ID (`p0-7-llm-seam`); merge = green CI, no other ceremony solo.

**Frontend** (fills in at Phase 1): TS strict; server state exclusively via TanStack Query keyed by table+hunt; no `useEffect` data fetching; Mantine components only (no bespoke CSS until something forces it). Component tests with `vitest` + Testing Library for anything with logic (score cell states, rubric widgets from `value_schema`); no snapshot tests.

## 3. Core Interfaces

*Status: proposal — every interface in this section.* Reviewed during the v1.1 critique and **deliberately left unhardened**: pinning `RunState` fields or client signatures before code exists would invert the authority order (code owns exact interfaces) and manufacture doc/code conflicts out of ordinary iteration. The critique's job was removing ambiguity a developer *must* resolve to proceed; these are ambiguities the code is supposed to resolve. When the real interfaces stabilize (expect: end of Phase 0), update this section to match them and note it in the changelog — or replace the bodies with pointers to the modules.

### RunState (`worker/src/manzil_worker/state.py`)

The current interface uses Source-claim and resolved-claim collections carrying `origin_key`, concrete target, applicability, `claim_group_id`, and candidate lineage. Exact fields live in `worker/src/manzil_worker/state.py`; the relevant P3-SC2 shape is:
```python
class RunState(BaseModel):
    job_id: UUID
    job_type: JobType                     # ingest | refresh | rescore | investigate
    mode: Literal["workflow", "agents"] = "workflow"
    url: str
    source_policy: SourcePolicy = "tiers_1_2_3"   # §10.7; read by PLAN + DISCOVER
    hunt_listing_id: UUID | None = None   # None in Phase 0 CLI runs
    plan: PlanManifest | None = None      # §10.4 contract shape
    cursor: int = 0                       # index into the stage list
    property_id: UUID | None = None
    sources: list[SourceState] = []       # url, tier_used, outcome, cleaned_path, hash
    source_claims: list[SourceClaim] = []
    resolved_claims: list[SourceClaim] = []
    floor_plans: list[FloorPlanIn] = []
    checkpoint: CheckpointPrompt | None = None           # §10.10 contract shape
    cost_usd: Decimal = Decimal("0")
```
`SourceClaim` carries `{criterion_key, value, confidence, evidence_quote, source_id, model, prompt_version, target_scope, floor_plan_ref, floor_plan_id, applicability, claim_group_id, origin_key, resolution_rule, disputed, candidate_claim_group_ids}`. `floor_plan_ref` is response-local and must resolve before persistence; `floor_plan_id` is the concrete database/runtime target.

### P3-SC scoped-fact persistence contract

*Status: implemented by P3-SC2 on 2026-07-21. This section records the selected mechanics and remains the current contract.*

Use the existing `extractions` name as one unified append-only fact store. Because the application is pre-live and resettable, add a new destructive pre-live migration that drops/rebuilds the dependent Extraction/Override objects with the final shape; do not add compatibility columns, dual writes, legacy views, or data backfills. Keep earlier migrations immutable, then validate the whole chain with `supabase db reset`. This is safer than editing migration history already applied to local environments and safer than preserving ambiguous old rows. The reset destroys local Extraction/Override data; fixture/dev seed recreation is the recovery path.

Required `extractions` mechanics:

- columns follow DESIGN §8.2: `record_kind`, non-null `origin_key`, nullable `source_id`, concrete `target_scope`, nullable `floor_plan_id`, nullable `applicability`, non-null `claim_group_id`, value/confidence/evidence/model, nullable resolution fields, `disputed`, `job_id`, and timestamp;
- checks enforce `target_scope='floor_plan'` iff `floor_plan_id is not null`; true Property facts require null applicability; exact Floor Plan rows require `specific_floor_plans`; generalized unit claims are Property targets with `all_units | select_units | unit_scope_unspecified`;
- `floor_plans` exposes `unique(id, property_id)` and `extractions(floor_plan_id, property_id)` uses a composite FK, so a scoped row cannot cross Properties;
- page candidates use `origin_key='property_source:' || source_id`; non-page producers use a stable namespaced key. One response claim mapped to several Floor Plans expands into separate rows sharing `claim_group_id`;
- candidate and resolved rows are distinguished by `record_kind`. A constraint trigger validates that `extraction_resolution_candidates.resolution_extraction_id` names a resolved row, its candidate IDs name candidate rows, and all rows share Property/Hunt/Criterion/concrete-target identity;
- separate partial global/hunt indexes are used wherever nullable `hunt_id` participates, rather than relying on ordinary NULL equality. They are intentionally non-unique because append-only history repeats the same current identity; the current views choose the newest version.

Centralized reads:

- `current_extraction_candidates` is a `security_invoker` view (and the repository's sole candidate-current query): candidate rows only, newest `(extracted_at desc, id desc)` per `(property_id, hunt scope using IS NOT DISTINCT FROM semantics, criterion_key, origin_key, target_scope, floor_plan_id)`. Applicability is data, not identity; a newer claim from the same origin/target supersedes its earlier applicability.
- `current_extractions` is the equivalent resolved-only view, newest per `(property_id, hunt scope, criterion_key, target_scope, floor_plan_id)`. Frontend, rescore, projection, filters, and future API reads consume this view/repository seam—never `ORDER BY extracted_at` plus first-per-Criterion logic.
- `extraction_resolution_candidates` is append-only with `(resolution_extraction_id, candidate_extraction_id)` primary key and `selected bool`; candidates are not embedded as opaque JSON.
- a successful complete Source refresh appends explicit unknown/not-found candidate observations for Source claims that disappeared, after Floor Plans have been resolved/upserted. Unchanged, partial, or failed refreshes do not retire truth. Persistence order is Floor Plans → candidates → resolutions/provenance → scores/projection → cursor advance.
- a one-Source run still writes one candidate plus one resolved row with `resolution_rule='single_source'`.

Overrides are rebuilt append-only with `(hunt_listing_id, criterion_key, target_scope, floor_plan_id, applicability, value, user_id, note, created_at)`. Current identity is Listing + Criterion + concrete target; use separate partial indexes for Property and Floor Plan targets. A null value is a revert tombstone for that target. v1 permits true-Property, all-units, and exact-Floor-Plan overrides; human select/unspecified corrections are intentionally absent.

### P3-SC3 Property Catalog and set-valued Rubric contract

*Status: implemented by P3-SC3 on 2026-07-22.*

`shared/src/manzil_shared/catalog.py` remains the source for Catalog seed and
sync SQL. It now generates the checked-in `supabase/seed.sql` and migration
`20260802000000_catalog_sync_p3_sc3.sql`; the Catalog contains 32 entries,
including the approved 13-Criterion Property tranche. `property` is a real
`CriterionCategory`, not a frontend-only group. Preference conveniences use
`available_sources_only` + `verified_positive_preferred`; `smoking_policy`
and the type Criteria retain their approved decision-relevant defaults.

Array values are JSON arrays with controlled item schemas, `minItems: 1`, and
`uniqueItems: true`. Schema generation carries those constraints through
Pydantic at runtime. The engine accepts only non-string sequences for both
fact and configured values; `contains_any` means non-empty set intersection,
and `contains_all` means the fact contains every configured member. Empty
configured sets, duplicates, wrong shapes, and scalar use of either operator
are validation failures/non-matches rather than permissive coercions. Exact
array equality is not exposed. The API validates array options against the
same Catalog JSON Schema; the Rubric editor uses a multi-select, warns on
overlapping sets without blocking the save, and groups Criteria by Catalog
category.

The Extraction schema still comes only from the Catalog and remains zero-tool.
It emits the Property tranche as Property claims. `FloorPlanIn.unit_types`
uses the same controlled vocabulary and persists to `floor_plans.unit_types`;
SCORE/rescore overlay the exact Floor Plan set, while Unit Group presentation
may show only the labeled union of member Floor Plans. Listing detail renders
Property facts once and Display-Floor-Plan facts separately.

`worker/fixtures/dev_rubric.v1.json` is a versioned, declarative, Gate-free
smoke Rubric with stable score goldens. `manzil seed-dev-rubric` owns one
deterministic development Hunt: missing state is installed, an identical
canonical Rubric is a true no-op, and local Rubric drift fails unless
`--force` is supplied. It is separate from the Phase 0 eval Rubric and is
never a production Hunt default. P3-SC4 owns the human-labeled scoped bench
and canonical recording refresh; the pre-SC3 synthetic seed/API recordings
are adapted only at their test helper seams with explicit `not_found` values.

### P3-SC4 scoped unit-claim contract

*Status: implementation landed 2026-07-27. The Owner waived the human
canonical-ten/current-pin acceptance tail as a P3-6 prerequisite on 2026-07-28.
The missing evidence remains technical debt; it is not recorded as passed.*

The dynamic zero-tool EXTRACT schema emits required sparse arrays for
`patio_balcony`, `private_entry`, `in_unit_laundry`, `parking`, `cooling`,
`dishwasher`, and `heating`; an empty list means Source silence. Each item has
one non-null raw claim value, confidence/evidence, Unit Applicability, and
response-local Floor Plan refs only for `specific_floor_plans`. Response keys
must be unique; exact refs must exist in that response. Boolean Criteria emit
one value per concrete target; laundry, parking, cooling, and heating emit one
claim per distinct typed value and may retain several positive variants. One claim may name several Floor Plans;
EXTRACT expands it to candidates sharing one `claim_group_id`.
The EXTRACT v8 prompt and generated schema clarify that applicability is claim
data while opted-in typed values are independent variants. Laundry's `none` means no laundry
option of any kind: a page that denies in-unit laundry while advertising a
shared facility emits one `on_site` claim. The schema boundary deterministically
drops only a redundant generalized `none` when exactly one generalized positive
laundry mode is present; competing positive modes and exact claims still fail
closed. If a model repeats another conflict, the corrective retry receives the
field-specific rule in its validation error.

Catalog `claim_value_schema` validates the raw Source value while
`value_schema` remains the Rubric/effective-score contract. Boolean exact/all
claims compose to `confirmed` or `none`; typed exact/all claims keep the raw
laundry/parking/cooling value. Select/unspecified positives compose to
`advertised_unconfirmed`, which the engine explicitly refuses as satisfaction
of a confirmed-feature Gate regardless of configured delta. Old
Property/no-applicability rows for this migrated tranche never participate in
presence composition. Heating follows the same applicability path per Floor
Plan and affects utility composition only when gas/electric is exact or
explicit all-units.

The local bench label schema now carries raw scoped claims and Source-local
targets plus P3-SC5-ready diagram association truth. The harness reports
scoped-claim accuracy, exact-target recall, and wrong exact associations;
`manzil bench-audit-scoped` requires exactly ten finished labels and coverage
for exact/all/select/unspecified, negative, missing, shared-plan, and
ambiguous/unambiguous diagrams. The current local audit finds 10 gradeable
legacy labels plus 11 unfinished skeletons, but no scoped claims or diagram
labels, so every new coverage case is missing. Those labels are human-only:
finish/review them, rerun the current model pin, and record zero wrong exact
associations plus the Gate regression report before marking P3-SC4 complete.

Migration `20260815000000_multi_value_scoped_claims.sql` adds
`claim_variant` to candidate/resolved current identities. Laundry, parking,
cooling, and heating variants persist and reconcile independently; their
effective values are controlled sets, while boolean scoped Criteria remain
scalar. Flooring uses a canonical set variant so independently scoped material
sets remain current together and exact/all sets union for scoring. Prompt v8
and the schema boundary normalize malformed exact claims
across every scoped Criterion: valid response refs survive; explicit
select/some/certain/varies wording becomes `select_units`; everything else
becomes `unit_scope_unspecified`. This only reduces specificity and never
invents a Floor Plan association.

Prompt-v8 record-mode bench after the flooring follow-up: 10 finished labels,
0 failures, 11 unfinished skeletons skipped, Criterion accuracy 0.8085, Gate
accuracy 0.95, unknown accuracy 0.8333, plan-field accuracy 0.9479, and 0 wrong
exact associations (`extract-v8-multi-scoped-claims-final.json`). The legacy
labels still contain no scoped-claim grading, so scoped accuracy/target recall
remain unavailable and the existing human-label debt is unchanged.

### P3-SC6/SC7 objective unit-feature contract

*Status: engineering landed 2026-07-29. Synthetic extraction, verification,
reconciliation, effective-value, scoring, Catalog/migration, and frontend
fixtures pass. The human canonical bench does not yet contain the per-Criterion
coverage required for an extraction-accuracy claim; that evidence remains
explicit rollout debt.*

`P3_SC6_KEYS` names the six boolean Source-claim Criteria. They join
`SCOPED_UNIT_CLAIM_KEYS`, use the existing sparse Source-local claim schema and
P3-6 resolution path, and compose through `PRESENCE_LIKE_KEYS` /
`BOOLEAN_PRESENCE_KEYS` to `confirmed | advertised_unconfirmed | none`.
No stage or persistence special-case bypasses reconciliation.

`flooring_materials` is also a sparse scoped claim, but it is deliberately not
presence-like. `CONFIRMED_SCOPE_ONLY_KEYS` tells the shared resolver to retain
the controlled array for exact and `all_units` claims while returning unknown
for select/unspecified claims. The raw resolved Extraction remains available
to the Floor Plan detail surface, which labels that evidence advertised and
shows its materials without feeding it to points or Gates.

The Catalog generator owns migration
`20260813000000_sc6_sc7_catalog.sql` and `supabase/seed.sql`. The Rubric editor
reuses the existing array widgets and option-overlap warning, plus a separate
warning whenever both `flooring_materials` and `flooring_quality` are enabled.
`manzil bench-audit-scoped` now reports the required SC6/SC7 cases per
Criterion; absent keys do not masquerade as a labeled missing case.

### P3-SC5 Floor Plan detail surface contract

*Status: item 7 (the detail surface) landed 2026-07-28; items 1–6 and 8–9 (the
diagram substrate) landed 2026-07-28 in three slices. P3-SC5 is functionally
complete; see "Outstanding" at the end of this section for what is not yet
evidenced.*

The detail surface is presentation-only: no contract, scoring, schema, or API
change. The substrate work adds config, a pure module, one CLI op, and a
Storage `delete`; the `floor_plan_images` schema itself already shipped with
P3-7a2, so no migration was required.

**Composition.** `FloorPlanList` replaces `FloorPlanPins` inside the drawer's
Floor plans card and owns the open-plan state. It renders one `FloorPlanCard`
per plan and one `FloorPlanDetailModal`. `floorPlanAmenities.ts` is the pure,
unit-tested seam between them: Catalog + resolved Extractions + active
Overrides in, four presence buckets out. Tranche membership is derived from the
Catalog — an entry participates when its effective `value_schema.enum` carries
`advertised_unconfirmed`; P3-SC7 adds the one controlled-set exception,
`flooring_materials`. Resolution goes through the existing `activeOverrides` /
`extractionForFloorPlan` seam. Exact/all flooring agrees with the score;
select/unspecified flooring is intentionally shown as advertised evidence while
remaining unknown to scoring for that concrete Floor Plan.

**Modal placement.** Desktop scopes the overlay to the drawer column by setting
`position: absolute; inset: 0` on the Modal `root`, `inner`, and `overlay`
slots against `Drawer.Body`, which now owns `position: relative`; the modal is
rendered with `withinPortal={false}` inside that body. Below `sm` the overrides
are dropped and `fullScreen` applies. This is consistent with DESIGN §9.4's
“nested modal above the Listing Drawer”, so it needed no §20 entry — but it is
a real branch, and both halves are pinned by tests.

**Reads.** `floor_plans.detail_url` / `source_native_id` (P3-SC2 columns) are
now surfaced and were added to the frontend `FloorPlan` type. Diagrams read the
already-shipped `current_floor_plan_images` associations exposed by
`usePropertyImages`, filtered to `kind = 'floor_plan_diagram'`. Until P3-SC5's
worker half populates those associations, every plan renders the explicit
“No floor plan diagram found” state — fail-open to a link-out, never a
substituted Property photo. The same rule removed diagrams from the drawer's
Property photo gallery.

**Rider fix.** `heating` was absent from the frontend's `SCOPED_UNIT_CRITERIA`
set, so its legacy Property/no-applicability rows still participated in
display resolution against this section's rule. Added.

#### Diagram substrate (items 1–6, 8–9)

**Discovery (item 1).** `discover_images` already carried alt/title/caption,
the containing Floor Plan card, `data-plan-id`, nearby labels, srcset (largest
variant only), and JSON-LD. Added: the enclosing `<a href>` when it points at
an image asset (`full_size_url`), suffix-checked so a *plan detail page* link
is not mistaken for a bigger copy of the image.

**Profile (item 2).** `normalize_image` and the new `normalize_diagram` share
one `_normalize` body; byte and decompression-bomb limits are
profile-independent — a diagram gets more pixels, never more trust. Diagrams
also get their own budget: `MAX_FLOOR_PLAN_DIAGRAMS_PER_PROPERTY` (30) and
`_PER_PLAN` (3), applied separately from `MAX_STORED_IMAGES` so photo ordering
cannot evict a diagram found late in a gallery. The IMAGE_FETCH round-robin
admits `2 * MAX_STORED_IMAGES + MAX_FLOOR_PLAN_DIAGRAMS_PER_PROPERTY`
candidates so failed/unusable photos have bounded replacement headroom and both
budgets can actually fill.

**Deterministic classification (item 3).** `enrich/diagram_signals.py` is a
pure predicate over structural evidence (containing card, native plan ID) and
the image's own accessible text. Filename resemblance is explicitly
insufficient. IMAGE_CLASSIFY remains the visual fallback; when it finds a
diagram the deterministic pass missed, `_renormalize_late_diagrams` re-fetches
and re-stores at the diagram profile — and on failure keeps the photo-profile
copy rather than losing the image.

**Association (items 4–5).** The schema shipped with P3-7a2. Three fixes: refs
now resolve through `floor_plan_ids_by_source_ref` keyed by the image's own
page (an image from Source B could previously link to Source A's plan);
`producer_job_id` resolves defensively like the Extraction writes; and a third
association rule was added after the corpus smoke showed real pages name the
plan in the **diagram's own `alt`** ("Floor plan Studio", "Savoy diagram")
rather than in a sibling label. That rule is diagram-only, requires the plan
name to appear in the text, ignores names under three characters, and resolves
overlaps by most-specific-wins; an exact tie stays unmatched.

**VISION exclusion (item 6).** Moved out of `select_kitchen_targets` into a
shared `eligible_quality_images` gate honouring both `kind` and the visual
`diagram` flag, so `flooring_quality` inherits it instead of re-deriving it.

**Lifecycle (item 8).** Image retirement is now **Source-local for all image
kinds** — the previous Property-wide sweep let a run over one Source retire
every other Source's images, a live bug for photos as well as diagrams.
Associations gain `unlink` rows on a complete Source refresh (partial or failed
discovery retires nothing). `_merge_into_canonical` re-points associations onto
the surviving canonical image before deleting placeholder duplicates, which the
FK cascade would otherwise strip. `split_property` carries associated images
across with their plans: the `floor_plan_images` trigger fires on UPDATE as
well as INSERT, so a cross-Property straddle cannot even be recorded and has to
be prevented. `manzil purge-images [--property] [--dry-run]` is the explicit
purge path; it removes only images that are non-current AND unassociated AND
uncited, and never deletes a Storage object another row still names.

**Legibility (item 9), profile now fixed.** The §7.5 comparison rendered a
2400 px sheet with 11 pt room labels and 8 pt dimension strings, softened and
noised to resemble a real scan, then encoded at five profiles:

| profile | stored | bytes | 8 pt text in asset | mean err vs lossless |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| photo 1024/q82 | 1024 | 5 KB | 10.2 px | 1.87 |
| 2048/q82 | 2048 | 14 KB | 20.5 px | 0.91 |
| 2048/q90 | 2048 | 34 KB | 20.5 px | 0.84 |
| 2048/q95 | 2048 | 106 KB | 20.5 px | 0.79 |
| 2048/lossless | 2048 | 412 KB | 20.5 px | 0 |

The **dimension** carries legibility (10.2 px → 20.5 px glyphs); the **quality**
axis does not — q82→q95 cut mean error 13% for 7.5× the bytes, and lossless
cost 29× for a difference invisible at these glyph sizes. Profile fixed at
`webp-2048-q82-v1`, correcting the provisional q95 the plan assumed. Storage
against the free-tier ceiling (§17 R9) decided the tie.

**Outstanding.** Two things are not evidenced and should not be treated as
passed:

1. *The item-7 visual pass is still partial.* The collapsed card and the
   modal's content were confirmed against seeded data at desktop width in dark
   mode; the modal's geometry after the `styles` fix, light mode, and the
   ~375px layout were never inspected — browser automation could not reliably
   reopen the drawer. Two tests pin the slot overrides and the mobile fork, but
   a test is not a look.
2. *`full_size_url` is unexercised on real pages.* No page in the local corpus
   wraps a diagram thumbnail in an anchor to an image asset, so that preference
   has synthetic coverage only. It is harmless when absent, but its real-world
   value is unproven.

Validation also exposed unrelated pre-existing Catalog drift: the authoritative
DESIGN §8.2 and pinned test/seed say studio `beds` delta `-0.5`, while the
committed Python Catalog says `-1.0`; several other committed Catalog defaults
also differ from the generated seed. P3-SC4 deliberately did not bury that
separate decision inside its six-key migration. Resolve the Catalog drift
explicitly before requiring the global Catalog generator guard to be green.

### P3-6 multi-Source reconciliation

*Status: landed 2026-07-28. The Owner-waived human scoped labels and the
stage-specific Gemini comparison remain technical debt, not passed evidence.*

New ingest manifests fan out the DISCOVER slate through a second idempotent
FETCH/EXTRACT pass, VERIFY each Source in isolation, then RECONCILE Property
and Source-local exact Floor Plan targets independently. `SourceResult` keeps
each Source's claims and Floor Plans isolated. Family-deduped voting follows
the §10.6 ladder: numeric tolerance, semantic equivalence, supermajority,
official arbiter, one tier/family-distinct sibling round, majority, then
conservative low-confidence fallback. Escalation inserts
`FETCH → EXTRACT → VERIFY → RECONCILE` after the current cursor and records
rounds in `plan.escalation`; the runner re-reads the manifest on every step, so
inserted stages are resumable and old manifests keep their original ordering.
Settled target identities never reopen.

Every Source candidate is appended independently, followed by one resolved
Extraction and explicit selected/unselected provenance edges. Shared claim
groups are resolved per concrete Floor Plan. A complete successful Source
refresh can append not-found replacements only for that Source's omitted
candidates; another Source's evidence remains current. `split_property`
detaches mixed provenance edges and appends a fresh resolution on both
Properties, so reversal cannot leave a cross-Property graph.

Gate-bearing disputes raise `resolve_dispute` after bounded escalation; the
default is **Leave unknown** and remains parked until P3-11 adds its timeout
sweep. Conservative fallback derives the Property-level **Problematic** badge;
candidate values, Source domains, confidence, and the resolution rule are
inspectable in Criterion evidence. Tasks History summarizes escalation and
retains the full manifest. P3-6 model-assisted calls use
`google/gemini-3-flash-preview`; DISCOVER stays on Haiku and VISION retains its
independent pin.

### Visit Checklist contract (DESIGN §9.7)

*Status: design ratified 2026-07-30 (VC-0). No code yet.*

The only subsystem here with **no pipeline and no LLM surface at all** — no stages,
no fixtures, no bench. It is API + DB + frontend, so the contract below is the
whole of its shared machinery.

**Entry identity.** `visit_entries` is append-only; the current value is the
newest `(created_at, id)` for
`(visit_id, item_key, visit_unit_id, owner_user_id)`, resolved **only** through
the `current_visit_entries` view — never an ad-hoc "latest item" query, for the
same reason scoped Extractions centralize theirs.

- `visit_unit_id is null` ⇔ the item is `property`-scoped.
- `owner_user_id is null` ⇔ shared item (Fact/Check/Question);
  non-null ⇔ Impression keyed to that member.
- `author_user_id` is always the writer, so shared items attribute without a
  second table.

**Scope resolution** is the template's, not the caller's: an item's
`visit_template_items.scope` decides whether a write carries a `visit_unit_id`.
The API rejects the mismatch rather than coercing it — a `property` item written
against a unit is a client bug, and silently accepting it produces exactly the
mis-filed answer the design exists to prevent.

**Sync payload.** One batch endpoint, `PUT /v1/visits/{id}/entries`, taking an
array where each element carries its own `prev_entry_id` (the row the author
believed current, or null for a first write). The write always lands. When the
identity's current row is no longer `prev_entry_id`, the fork is visible through
`visit_entry_conflicts` (identities with more than one row sharing a non-null
`prev_entry_id`); resolving appends a row whose `prev_entry_id` is the chosen
one, collapsing it. Only Facts and Checks can fork.

**Template versioning.** `visits.template_version` freezes at creation.
Completion percentages and section membership resolve against that version, so
editing the template never rewrites a finished Visit's meaning.

**Template source of truth.** A Python module in the API package, seeded into
`visit_template_items` by migration, with a parity test asserting table matches
module — the arrangement `criteria_catalog` uses. It must not go in `shared/`,
which stays domain-blind.

**Roll-up.** `visit_unit_group_scores`, a `security_invoker` view keyed
`(hunt_listing_id, unit_group_key)`: mean of unit-scope Impressions per unit,
latest Visit per unit, then max across units in the group. A view rather than a
projection until it measurably hurts; `all_in_components` is the precedent for
promoting it later.

### Stage protocol and runner (`runner.py`)
```python
Stage = Callable[[RunState, StageCtx], Awaitable[RunState]]
# StageCtx: db, llm, fetcher, clock, tracer — injected, so tests fake them wholesale
```
Runner behavior (DESIGN §10.2): persist state → advance cursor → heartbeat `locked_at`; on `CheckpointRaised` persist prompt and park as `waiting_user`; on `StageRetryable` retry with backoff per the tunables table.

### LLM client seam (`llm/client.py`)
```python
async def call_structured(stage: str, schema: type[T], content: str | list[Block]) -> T: ...
async def call_agent(stage: str, task: str, tools: list[Tool], max_turns: int = 8) -> AgentResult: ...
async def call_vision(stage: str, schema: type[T], images: list[ImageRef]) -> T: ...
```
The seam resolves model + prompt from `llm/config.py` by `stage`, routes all live calls through OpenRouter's OpenAI-compatible API (forced-tool structured output, `cache_control` on stable prefixes, upstream provider pinning), emits the Langfuse trace, tallies cost onto the active `RunState`, and honors `MANZIL_LLM_MODE` (record/replay, §5). Nothing else imports the SDK.

### Tunables (`shared/config.py` — single home, no magic numbers in stage code)

The single-home rule itself is **settled**; every *value* in this table is a starting point expected to be tuned against Phase 0 reality (tune freely, changelog-note the change).

| Name | Initial value | Where used |
|---|---|---|
| `EVIDENCE_FUZZY_THRESHOLD` | 85 (rapidfuzz partial_ratio) | VERIFY check 1 |
| `RENT_AGREE_PCT` / `SQFT_AGREE_PCT` | 3% / 5% | RECONCILE rung 2 |
| `PLAUSIBILITY_COLD_START_N` | 8 listings per metro | VERIFY check 3 |
| `STAGE_RETRIES` / backoff | 3, `10s · 2^attempt` jittered | runner |
| `JOB_ORPHAN_AFTER` | 5 min without heartbeat | queue reclaim |
| `AGENT_MAX_TURNS` | 8 | P3 loops |
| `CHECKPOINT_TIMEOUT` | 24 h | scheduler sweep |
| `MAX_STORED_IMAGES` / `MAX_IMAGE_CLASSIFY_IMAGES` / `MAX_GALLERY_PHOTOS` / `MAX_VISION_TARGET_IMAGES` | 120 / 120 / 30 / 8 | retained candidate photos / cheap ≤384 px classification / eligible visible gallery / total quality-target cap |
| `IMAGE_CLASSIFY_BATCH_SIZE` | 30 | deterministic bounded ONNX batches within the 120-candidate Property pass |
| `VISION_TARGET_QUOTAS` | `{"kitchen_quality": 3}` | deterministic per-profile quality selection; future intended split 3 kitchen / 3 flooring / 2 bathroom |
| `IMAGE_CLASSIFY_MAX_DIM` | 384 px | in-memory ONNX classifier thumbnails |
| `MAX_FLOOR_PLAN_DIAGRAMS_PER_PLAN` / `MAX_FLOOR_PLAN_DIAGRAMS_PER_PROPERTY` | 3 / 30 | P3-SC5 diagram association/property caps; separate from ordinary photo/VISION caps |
| `IMAGE_MAX_DIM` | 1024 px | IMAGE_FETCH normalization |
| `IMAGE_MAX_DOWNLOAD_BYTES` / `IMAGE_MAX_PIXELS` | 20 MiB / 40 Mpx | IMAGE_FETCH rejection bounds |
| `IMAGE_WEBP_QUALITY` / `IMAGE_FETCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 82 / 20 s | IMAGE_FETCH normalization/download |
| `FETCH_MIN_BODY_BYTES` | 5 000 | outcome classifier |
| `RENT_PLAUSIBLE_MIN` / `RENT_PLAUSIBLE_MAX` | 400 / 10 000 | VERIFY check 3 cold-start static band |
| `SQFT_PER_BED_MIN` / `SQFT_PER_BED_MAX` | 250 / 2 500 | VERIFY check 3 sqft-per-bed ratio band |
| `DEPOSIT_MAX_RENT_MULTIPLIER` | 2.0 | VERIFY check 3 (deposit ≤ ~2× rent) |
| `CLEANED_TEXT_MIN_CHARS` | 800 | outcome classifier (positive-content floor) |
| `SHELL_SCRIPT_RATIO` | 0.7 | outcome classifier (JS-shell signature) |
| `TIER2_MIN_DELAY_SECONDS` | 3.0 | tier-2 per-domain politeness |
| `TIER3_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 90.0 | unblocker request timeout (vendor solves challenges server-side) |
| `EMBEDDED_SCRIPT_MIN_CHARS` | 500 | structured-data miner (candidate state-blob floor) |
| `EMBEDDED_DATA_MAX_CHARS` | 40 000 | structured-data miner (digest cap in cleaned text) |

---

## 4. Prompt Management

*Status: settled.*

Prompts live as markdown files in `worker/src/manzil_worker/llm/prompts/{stage}.md` with a tiny front-matter header (`id`, `version`, `cacheable_prefix_marker`). The client loads by stage, splits at the marker — everything above it is the stable prefix sent with `cache_control` (schema text, rules, few-shots); everything below is per-call. `prompt_version` is recorded on every extraction row and every Langfuse trace, which is what makes the §8 prompt-change runbook enforceable. Prompt files are code: reviewed in PRs, never edited in place without a version bump.

---

## 5. Testing and Fixtures

*Status: settled.*

**Corpus layout** — one directory per saved listing page:
```
worker/tests/fixtures/corpus/{domain}--{slug}/
    raw.html        # as saved from the browser
    cleaned.txt     # produced by the cleaner (regenerate when cleaner changes)
    meta.json       # url, saved_at, is_official, notes
```
**Bench** — `fixtures/bench/manifest.md` names the canonical 10 frozen cases and why; `fixtures/bench/labels/{slug}.json` holds hand-labeled ground truth for Gate-bearing Criteria + rent/fees (DESIGN §19 Phase 0). P3-SC4 landed the scoped label/harness contract and `manzil bench-audit-scoped`; the human labels must now finalize those ten cases so the set includes exact, all-unit, select-unit, unqualified, negative, missing, shared-plan, and ambiguous/unambiguous diagram evidence. Do not infer labels from old outputs. The 2026-07-27 audit found 10 gradeable legacy labels plus 11 unfinished skeletons and no coverage for any new scoped/diagram case. Remaining estimate: 6–10 human labeling hours and 2–4 h to run and interpret the current-pin baseline.

**Local eval kit (DESIGN §20 v2.8)** — `corpus/` and `bench/labels/` are **gitignored** (scraped third-party pages with embedded vendor keys; labels are ground truth about those exact local snapshots). Only `.gitkeep` placeholders and `bench/manifest.md` are tracked. CI reads the committed synthetic `fixtures/pages/` only; the corpus sweep test skips when the corpus is empty. Back the kit up (§8) — delisted/hostile pages cannot be re-fetched.

**Record/replay** — `MANZIL_LLM_MODE=record` runs live and writes each call's request-hash → response to `fixtures/recorded/`. The hash covers `(stage, model_id, prompt_version, sha256(content))` — deliberately *not* the raw request object, so SDK upgrades and parameter reordering don't invalidate the cache, while any change that could alter model output does; `replay` (the CI default) serves from disk and **fails on any miss** — CI can never silently call a live model. Golden tests in `shared/tests/golden/` assert exact score breakdowns per the §9.3 contract; a change to the engine that alters any golden requires updating the golden *in the same PR with an explanation*.

---

## 6. Observability

*Status: settled.*

Langfuse initialized inside the client seam only. Trace naming: `{job_type}/{stage}`, session = `job_id`; metadata on every span: `mode`, `model`, `prompt_version`, `listing_slug` (bench) and token/cost figures. The eval harness reads its numbers *from Langfuse traces*, not from ad-hoc accounting — one source of truth for spend and latency in both modes.

---

**Sequencing:** row order is the default dependency order, not a mandate — tasks without a data dependency parallelize freely (frontend and API rows in Phase 1 are two independent tracks after P1-4). ### Phase 1 — replace the spreadsheet (single user) — exited 2026-07-10

Revised on entry against `.claude/plans/phase-1-frontend-api.md` (the Phase 1 plan document — read it for full directory trees, sketches, and sequencing; this table records the decisions it settled). Key cross-cutting calls the plan made, referenced by short names below: **API layout** — domain-oriented package layout + chain-of-dependencies adapted from fastapi-best-practices, *not* SQLAlchemy/ORM (IMPLEMENTATION §2's DB-access rule stands: `service.py` wraps `supabase-py`/PostgREST); **ownership stand-in** — `current_user.id == hunt.owner_id` in place of the Phase 2 role matrix, shaped so P2-1/P2-2 change only the dependency body; **rescore-on-mutation** — every scoring-input mutation (rubric PUT, settings PATCH, override POST, fee POST/PATCH) ends its service function with one `rescore` job INSERT, never a direct recompute; **two-client frontend** — `supabase-js` direct reads for continuously-rendered table data, a typed `apiClient` (codegen via `openapi-typescript`, committed output) for every mutation plus the one polled read (`GET /v1/hunts/{id}/jobs`).

### Phase 2 — collaboration

Current mechanics (superseding the phase-entry snapshot below): P2-5 replaces
all Jobs polling with one Hunt-scoped Supabase Realtime channel
over the five DESIGN §13.3 tables. Events invalidate the existing TanStack Query
prefixes; RLS remains the subscription boundary. P2-1..P2-5 are complete.

P2-6 adds direct RLS reads for `hunt_members`, comments, and ratings; API
mutations enforce author/self attribution. `hunt_members.display_name` supplies
the frontend-readable identity, comments soft-delete author-only, ratings are
constrained to 1–5 in half-star steps, and member colors accept the ordered tokens or `#RRGGBB`.
P2-1..P2-6 are complete.

P2-7 exposes nullable `jobs.plan` on `JobResponse` and adds Active/History tabs.
History reads terminal Jobs through the existing API and lazily reads each run's
`job_events` directly under RLS; filters compose by Listing, member, and outcome,
and Hunt-level runs render as `system`. Terminal Jobs are API-ordered by
`finished_at desc, created_at desc`; plan and timeline each expand independently.
P2-1..P2-7 are complete.

DESIGN v3.5 adds `listing_unit_group_states` (migration 20260725000000): one
Owner/Curator-managed row per Listing + stable Unit Group key, direct Hunt-wide
RLS read in the Overview, Realtime invalidation, and a PATCH upsert endpoint.
The same migration adds nullable `properties.city`; DEDUPE's existing geocode
seam reads Google's `locality` address component and terminal projection fills
it only when null. Existing Properties stay Unknown until confidently backfilled.
The Overview predicate registry now composes score range, rent/sqft/beds/baths,
city, Interest Status, visited, and availability without server pagination.
Rubric `MatchOp` adds `lte`/`gte`; the shared engine remains the sole evaluator,
and the API is authoritative for pure-bonus derivation.

Revised on entry against `.claude/plans/phase-2-collaboration.md` (the Phase 2 plan document — read it for the full policy matrix, endpoint tables, sequencing, and rationale; this table records the decisions it settled). Key cross-cutting calls, referenced by short names below: **membership helper** — one `security definer` function `private.member_role(hunt_id)` backs every policy (kills `hunt_members` policy recursion, keeps policy bodies one-liners); **own semantics** — "own listing/job" = `hunt_listings.added_by = auth.uid()`; hunt-level jobs (rescore, no `hunt_listing_id`) are Owner-managed; no new `jobs` column; **privileged surface** — `privileged.py` contains exactly four narrow collaboration ops (email-invite accept, invite email via `auth.admin`, Invitation Link join, owner transfer), everything else stays on the user-JWT client; **constrained database capabilities** — an `AFTER INSERT` trigger installs the Owner membership atomically, while authenticated-only `public.submit_listing` and `public.answer_job_checkpoint` RPCs independently enforce caller authority and commit multi-table mutations atomically without granting client table-write access to global facts or `job_events`; **real-JWT tests** — conftest mints four real users (owner/curator/member/outsider) via the admin API so permission tests exercise actual RLS, and CI gains the local Supabase stack; **matrix-in-RLS** — `WITH CHECK` encodings make even the sharp §4.2 rows database-enforced (self color edits can't change `role`; curator can answer a parked checkpoint but not cancel the job); **member colors** — automatic invite assignment uses `dusk, clay, moss, ochre, brick, olive, stone, plum`, while P2-6 accepts those tokens or canonical `#RRGGBB`. P2-1..P2-8 and P2-11 are complete (changelog 2.0.28–2.0.35, 2.0.42–2.0.45); P2-9's manual pass and P2-10's real-use checks were confirmed 2026-07-17; **CI went green and Yusuf recorded the exit sign-off 2026-07-18 (2.0.55, DESIGN §20) — Phase 2 is closed.**

### Visits workstream — `VC-0`…`VC-8` (DESIGN §9.7, §19, §20 v3.30)

Parallel to Phase 3 and **gating nothing**: no pipeline dependency, no LLM
surface, and Phase 3's exit criteria are unaffected by its state. Ruled a
separate workstream rather than `P3-24…` precisely so it cannot read as a Phase 3
exit condition. Visit **photos** are a separate later *Visits phase 2*
(DESIGN §18) and are deliberately absent from v1 — the UI omits the affordance
rather than showing a control that does nothing.

Slice order and blocking: `VC-0` gates everything (DESIGN owns intent; code
before ratification inverts that). `VC-1` gates all implementation. `VC-3` is
the hinge — `VC-4`, `VC-5`, `VC-6`, `VC-7`, `VC-8` each depend on it and on
nothing else, so after `VC-3` they may proceed in any order or in parallel.

#### P3-SC1 Extraction-consumer audit (2026-07-21)

This was the migration checklist for P3-SC2 and is retained as the completed audit trail. The “Current location / assumption” column records the pre-migration state; every “Required P3-SC2 change” below landed in 2.0.71. Production searches now find raw `extractions`/`overrides` reads only at the centralized persistence seam or intentional history/lifecycle operations; current-value consumers read the security-invoker views/repository.

| Consumer | Current location / assumption | Required P3-SC2 change |
|---|---|---|
| Base schema/indexes | `supabase/migrations/20260705000000_global_tables.sql`, `20260708000000_hunt_and_pipeline_tables.sql`: append-only but latest identity stops at Criterion | New destructive pre-live migration rebuilds unified store, provenance join, scoped Overrides, composite same-Property FK, partial indexes, current views; reset only, no backfill |
| RLS | `20260714000000_rls_policies.sql`: global Catalog vs Hunt custom row policy | Apply identical privacy to candidates, resolutions, provenance join and security-invoker views; Floor Plan/image metadata global-authenticated, bytes signed only through a Hunt containing the Property |
| Ingest persistence | `worker/src/manzil_worker/queue.py::_persist_ingest_results`: writes `state.reconciled` and auxiliary Extraction blocks before Floor Plan upsert | Resolve/upsert Floor Plans first; persist candidate rows and shared claim groups, then resolved rows + joins, then score/projection; auxiliary producers use stable `origin_key`s and scoped targets |
| ENRICH persistence | `worker/src/manzil_worker/queue.py` enrichment refresh writes grocery proximity as a latest Criterion row | Write candidate + resolution through the same fact repository with a stable producer origin and correct Property target |
| Pipeline state/schema | `worker/src/manzil_worker/state.py`, `stages/extract.py`, `llm/schema_gen.py`: Criterion-keyed `extractions`/`reconciled`; one value per Criterion | Introduce sparse Source claims with response-local Floor Plan keys, applicability and evidence; expand only after Source-local identity resolution; preserve full candidate lineage |
| VERIFY/SCORE | `stages/verify.py` iterates Criterion buckets; `stages/score.py` selects the first value and overlays Floor Plan columns | Verify target/applicability evidence and same-Property identity; SCORE consumes one shared effective-value resolver implementing override/exact/all/generalized precedence |
| Rescore | `worker/src/manzil_worker/stages/rescore.py::_latest_catalog_extractions`, `_latest_hunt_extractions`, `_resolve_effective_values` use `DISTINCT ON` Criterion | Delete local latest logic; read `current_extractions`, scoped current Overrides and the shared effective resolver for each Floor Plan |
| Frontend Extraction query | `frontend/src/features/listings/api.ts::useExtractions` orders newest-first and keeps the first Criterion | Query the resolved current view (plus candidate provenance on detail demand), key by Criterion + concrete target, never flatten plan targets |
| Detail/Overview/filter/Compare | Listing detail and display helpers consume `Map<criterion_key, Extraction>` or score breakdown as though one value describes all units | Render Property facts once, Display-Floor-Plan effective facts per row, variation/applicability labels and quiet disagreement; Floor Plan modal consumes its target plus inherited generalized facts |
| Frontend contract notes/types | `frontend/API_ASSUMPTIONS.md` and `frontend/src/features/listings/types.ts` pin a latest-per-Criterion hand-typed shape | Regenerate/document the scoped resolved-view contract; type concrete target/applicability/provenance and eliminate the single-key map assumption |
| Overrides/API | Current table/routes/rescore identify latest Override only by Listing + Criterion | Add target/applicability validation and partial-current indexes; API accepts only true-Property, all-units or exact-plan v1 forms and confirms plan belongs to Listing's Property |
| Property merge/split | `queue._merge_into_canonical` rewrites all Extractions by Property; `worker/src/manzil_worker/ops/split_property.py` moves Source rows | Move candidate/resolution/provenance graphs without cross-Property Floor Plan references; split by Source lineage/origin, recompute resolutions, and verify associations/images |
| Refresh/image lifecycle | current image flow already distinguishes complete vs partial, but ordinary image replacement/deletion assumptions do not encode Floor Plan associations | Reuse authoritative-success semantics for claims/plans/diagram associations; diagram rows retire only on complete Source refresh; never delete append-only fact history |
| Shared models/catalog/scoring | `shared` Extraction/Criterion/Rubric models and operators encode scalar/latest semantics | Add domain-blind scope/applicability/policy vocabulary and `contains_any/all`; keep rental seed values outside the pure engine; extend exact breakdown goldens |
| Eval runner/harness | `worker/src/manzil_worker/evals/corpus_run.py` passes Criterion-keyed state through; `evals/harness.py` grades `[0]` per Criterion | Extend result/label contracts to Source-local Floor Plan keys, applicability, shared claims and abstention; grade target association separately from value accuracy and report raw zero-tolerance errors |
| Tests/fixtures/dev seed | queue projection, rescore, persistence snapshots, RLS matrix, synthetic pages and local bench encode old shape | Update all in one tranche; add target/applicability/RLS/refresh/split goldens, finalize ten-case bench, and add versioned `worker/fixtures/dev_rubric.v1.json` only in P3-SC3 |

Audit conclusion: the change is cross-cutting but bounded. Centralizing both current reads and effective-value composition is mandatory; leaving even one consumer on first-per-Criterion behavior would recreate the original oversight in a less visible place.

### Admin workstream — `AD-G`, `AD-C`, `AD-0`…`AD-5` (DESIGN §4.2, §19, §20 v3.37–v3.39)

Parallel to Phase 3 and **gating nothing**, for the same reason Visits is: an
operator surface is not agent-system work, and Phase 3's exit criteria are
unaffected by its state. Ruled a separate workstream rather than `P3-25…`
precisely so it cannot read as a Phase 3 exit condition.

Slice order and blocking: `AD-C` and `AD-G` deliberately ran **ahead of**
`AD-0`, because neither builds the panel — each fixes a present-tense defect
(spend the system could not see; history the system was destroying) and each
stands alone. `AD-0` then gates `AD-1`, which gates everything after it.
`AD-F` is a pure derived read over `AD-G`'s tables and needs only `AD-G`.

**The workstream is complete (`AD-C`, `AD-G`, `AD-0`…`AD-5`).**

Deferred to an **Admin phase 2** (DESIGN §18): enforcing spend caps that pause a
Hunt's queue, true user impersonation, and a general-purpose audit log over
non-admin actions.

**Two Supabase advisor findings on these objects are accepted, not oversights.**
Do not "fix" them without reading this:

- `rls_enabled_no_policy` on `site_admins` and `admin_audit_log` — RLS is on with
  *zero* policies, which denies every authenticated client outright. That is the
  intent: the admin roster must not be enumerable and the audit log must not be
  readable by its own subjects. Both are read through the service-role router.
  It is the same posture `feedback` has shipped with since P3-16.
- `pg_graphql_*_table_exposed` — every one of the 58 objects in the schema
  carries these; they report GraphQL schema discoverability, not an RLS gap.

anonymous visitor can create an account. It uses the existing Site Admin and

path.

### 7.x Demo Mode (`DM`) — entered 2026-08-05, parallel to Phase 3, gating nothing

Public viewability behind one shared read-only account (DESIGN §20 v3.53, §2.4, §16).
Plan: `.claude/plans/soft-finding-newell.md`. Two things a contributor must not
misread. **DM-1 is not a demo task** — it closes a cross-hunt read exposure that exists
today and is worth landing on its own merits; the demo is only what made it urgent.
And **nothing here is internet-reachable until DM-8 passes**: the build order puts every
enforcement control before the first byte of public exposure, deliberately.

Ownership transfer immediately darkens a release unless its publisher is still the current owner; historical publications do not block Hunt/account deletion. ## 8. Runbooks

*Status: settled.*

**Add a catalog criterion:** edit `shared/catalog.py` → regenerate `supabase/seed.sql` → `supabase db reset` → extraction schema picks it up automatically → add golden covering it → if gate-eligible, add a bench label field.

**Modify an existing criterion:** *widening* (new enum value, relaxed bound) — edit `catalog.py`, regenerate (+ `catalog_sync` migration post-Phase 1), update the golden; history and rubrics stay valid. *Narrowing* (removed value, tightened bound) — (1) impact-query nonconforming extractions and affected rubric options first; (2) define the value mapping; (3) one migration: upsert schema → **append** superseding extraction rows with the mapped values (`resolution_rule='schema_migration'`; never rewrite append-only history) → rewrite affected rubric options → bump touched hunts' `rubric_version` → enqueue their rescores; (4) re-run the bench (schema changes are output-affecting); (5) DESIGN §20 entry, mandatory. *Type changes* — never in place: retire the old key (disable in rubrics, keep history), add a new key. Post-Phase 1 deploy order for any of these: `supabase db push` **before** rolling services — old-worker/new-DB skew is benign, the reverse is not.

**Change a prompt or model:** bump `version` in the prompt front-matter (or model pin in `llm/config.py` — OpenRouter slugs like `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`) → run the bench in replay-invalidating mode (`record` against bench set) → compare report to previous → commit prompt + report together (recordings stay local — they're keyed to the gitignored corpus, DESIGN §20 v2.8). No eyeball-only prompt merges (DESIGN §6).

**Record new fixtures:** save page HTML into a new corpus dir → run cleaner → `MANZIL_LLM_MODE=record uv run --package worker pytest -k <slug>`.

**Back up the eval kit (after every labeling/saving session):** the corpus + labels are gitignored (DESIGN §20 v2.8) and unrecoverable if lost — delisted pages 404 and hostile pages need a working tier 3. One command, output goes anywhere private (external drive, private bucket — never this repo):
```bash
tar czf ~/manzil-eval-kit-$(date +%Y%m%d).tgz -C worker/tests/fixtures corpus bench/labels
```

**Manually requeue an orphaned job:** `update jobs set state='queued', locked_by=null, locked_at=null where id=… and state='running';` — safe at any stage boundary by construction.

**Apply migrations to hosted Supabase (Phase 1+):** `supabase link` once, `supabase db push`; never edit applied migrations, always add.

**Catalog changes on hosted DB:** `supabase/seed.sql` is local-reset-only. When `shared/catalog.py` changes after Phase 1, the generator also emits an idempotent upsert migration (`NNNN_catalog_sync.sql`) — catalog rows reach production the same way schema does, and drift between code seed and hosted rows is structurally impossible.

**Deploy (Phase 1+):** frontend — CF Pages builds `frontend/dist`; API — run
Uvicorn with `MANZIL_WORKER_INPROCESS=true` (the default), so the same service
also owns the worker loop. Hosting configuration and secrets stay outside the
repo until a deployment target is selected. A separate worker definition does
not exist yet and is added only by optional P3-1.

**Deploy the required ONNX classifier:** install the API with
`uv sync --frozen --package manzil-api --extra vision-onnx --no-dev`, place the
pinned `model.onnx` + `manifest.json` in an immutable build-time directory, and
set `MANZIL_IMAGE_CLASSIFY_ONNX_DIR` to it. Keep Uvicorn at one process;
the worker runs inference in a short-lived subprocess and streams one image at
a time. The generated artifact remains outside Git. ONNX authority is fixed by
DESIGN v3.52; missing configuration fails IMAGE_CLASSIFY and never selects the
retained LLM path. Full commands, telemetry, migration, and rollback live in
[`docs/onnx-image-classification.md`](docs/onnx-image-classification.md).

**Move the worker out of the API process (optional P3-1):** do not perform this
merely because Phase 3 starts. First confirm a DESIGN §5 trigger, then follow the
full [worker-isolation migration guide](docs/worker-isolation.md). The cutover
invariant is exactly one active claimant. Validate the standalone process in
staging first; in production, disable the API claimant before enabling the new
claimant. The resulting short no-claimant interval is safe because queued Jobs
are durable.

**Complete P3-7 VISION:** the deterministic image substrate is live but ratings
are fail-closed until the human reference asset is approved. Follow the full
[P3-7 VISION completion guide](docs/p3-7-vision-guide.md) for the manifest,
prompt/schema, bench, migration, gallery, retention, and rollout gates.

---

## 9. Changelog

The current entry is pinned first; older entries retain their existing append
history. Version and date, rather than row position, define chronology.

| Version | Date | Change |
|---|---|---|
| 2.0.181 | 2026-08-12 | **`management_reviews` option editing fixed (DESIGN v3.98).** `OptionMatchEditor` dispatched its operator list and value widget off `value_schema.type`, which is `"object"` for this one criterion; `>`/`<`/`=` fell through `selectWidget` to `EnumWidget` (its no-`enum` catch-all), rendering an empty, unusable `<Select>` even though the stored numeric value was valid. `range` accidentally worked because that op hardcodes two `NumberWidget`s regardless of schema type, but without the rating field's 1–5 bounds. Fixed by factoring v3.97's inline object→rating unwrap into a shared `comparableSchema()` export in `rubricDraft.ts` and resolving `OptionMatchEditor`'s schema through it once, up front. frontend 908 passed, 1 new regression test in `CriterionCard.test.tsx`; 2 pre-existing failures in `AdminJobsPage`/`AdminPeoplePage` are unrelated (concurrent uncommitted edits from another session — not touched); tsc clean for all touched files. |
| 2.0.180 | 2026-08-12 | **Rubric save-time validation, per-card dirty/revert, sticky save bar (DESIGN v3.97).** `put_rubric` fixed two bugs that broke essentially every rubric save: `_validate_option_schema` now validates `management_reviews`-shaped object criteria against `properties.rating` (matching the scoring engine's `_comparable()`) instead of the full object schema, and disabled criteria's options are no longer validated at all (matching what `get_rubric` actually returns to the engine). The frontend's mirrored `rubricDraft.ts` validator gets the same explicit object/rating handling — previously it only "passed" by an untyped fallthrough. `RubricEditor` moved its `value_schema` check off the render path: it now runs once on Save (top summary + inline per-card issues) instead of on every keystroke, which — combined with memoizing `CriterionCard` and stabilizing the editor's per-item callbacks — fixes a several-hundred-millisecond mobile input lag that never reproduced on desktop (every keystroke had been re-rendering and re-validating every card on the page). Each card gained a **Modified** indicator with a one-criterion revert-to-saved, and the editor gained a sticky bottom save bar reusing `SettingsSaveBar` (the same component Hunt Settings/Account use) listing which criteria are dirty. api 544 passed (2 new); frontend 906 passed (7 new); tsc clean. |
| 2.0.179 | 2026-08-11 | **Deleted Job spend moves to Statistics; Job deletion is click-confirmed.** Overview no longer fetches Statistics or renders a persistent deleted-spend banner. Statistics labels the retained-spend card **Deleted Job spend**. Tasks History and Admin Job modals retain named-subject and consequence disclosure but use an enabled `Delete Job` confirmation rather than typed Job text; genuinely permanent deletion paths remain typed. |
| 2.0.178 | 2026-08-11 | **Product email defaults off; tighter phone nav drawer.** Migration `20260901000025_disable_notification_defaults.sql` makes missing account/Hunt preferences resolve to disabled without changing existing explicit choices; API/demo fallbacks and notification tests match. The primary mobile navigation drawer padding is 6 px (60% of Mantine `xs`). |

| 2.0.158 | 2026-08-11 | **The tier-3 provider seam stops impersonating the target, and its configuration is all-or-nothing (DESIGN v3.78/v3.79).** Diagnosed from a production `ingest` job: every Zillow submission failed `FETCH: fetch error for <url> (status 400)`, and the 400 was Bright Data's API refusing the request — `BRIGHTDATA_ZONE` carried the API key's name instead of the zone's — so no page was ever fetched and the status belonged to nobody the message named. `fetching/tier3.py`: providers gain an `unwrap` alongside `build`; Bright Data now requests `format: "json"` and reads the `{status, headers, body}` envelope, so `FetchResult.status_code`/`headers` are the **target's** (a non-envelope 200 still degrades to the page, so an unexpected vendor shape is not read as a rejection), and `FetchResult` gains `provider`. A rejection of our own request raises the new `manzil_shared.errors.FetchProviderError` (provider, status, reason capped at `PROVIDER_ERROR_MAX_CHARS`=400, `retryable`), logged as `tier3_provider_rejected`; `record_fetch` is no longer called on that path, so a refusal costs no credit while a target 403 still does. `stages/fetch.py` maps it to `StageFatal` (400/401/402/403) or `StageRetryable` (429/5xx) per source, so sibling isolation is unchanged, and the target-error message now names the rung, the provider and the page: *the page returned status 500 at tier 1*. Config: `env_value()` treats blank/whitespace env vars as unset, `_Provider.required_env` becomes groups of aliases (`BRIGHTDATA_ZONE` | `BRIGHTDATA_KEY_NAME`, no default), `tier3_configured()` requires every group and warns `tier3_partially_configured` when it drops a half-configured provider, new `provider_configured()` backs Admin → System for both vendors, and the `source unfetchable` message names the missing settings. New `api/probe_logging.py` filters successful `/v1/health` and `/v1/ready` lines out of `uvicorn.access` (`MANZIL_LOG_PROBE_REQUESTS=true` restores them) — Render owns the probe cadence, we own the log. Tests: `test_tier3.py` (envelope, target status survival, provider rejection, congestion, missing zone, alias, blank env, non-envelope 200), new `test_stage_fetch_provider_errors.py` (fatal/retryable split, sibling isolation, message shape), `test_fetch_cost.py` (a refusal bills nothing), `test_probe_logging.py`. **Operational follow-up, not code:** prod must set `BRIGHTDATA_ZONE` to the real zone name before Zillow/Trulia/Realtor/apartments.com/forrent submissions can work — those domains are pinned at tier 3 in `fetch_adapter_registry`, so they go straight to the unblocker. |

Both guards now short-circuit on `isDemo()`. **Defect 2 (email invites):** `privileged.send_invite_email` sends through Supabase Auth with `should_create_user=False`, so an address with **no account** (FR13) is refused and the API returns 502 `invite_email_failed` — *after* the invite row is written. Seed: `reconcile_demo_principal()` deletes the stale singleton *before* inserting, so an installation predating v3.55 can be upgraded at all, reports a stranded Auth account rather than silently orphaning it, and refuses a principal that has one (**H1**). Tombstones carry **no foreign keys**: an FK cascades the tombstone away with the row it commemorates, and `via_cascade` lets a reader collapse one Hunt deletion and its 200 Listings into a single line. The subtle hazard, covered by test: a membership row dying inside a Hunt cascade must not write history whose FK parent is already gone. Visibility splits deliberately — **data provenance is shared with members, governance history belongs to the Owner** — because any member may reasonably ask who zeroed a fee on their shared Listing, while who demoted whom is a decision about the Hunt. Explicitly **not** a general-purpose audit log: Manzil already keeps this history correctly in `overrides`, `visit_entries` and the extraction lineage, and a parallel copy that can disagree with the domain table is a liability, not a safeguard. **Verification:** 8 new api tests against direct writes; api **258 passed** (1 unrelated pre-existing e2e failure from in-flight scoring work, confirmed by re-running with these triggers disabled) |
| 2.0.105 | 2026-08-01 | **Tier-3 fetching is priced, and Job cost is split by what bought it (AD-C, DESIGN v3.37).** `jobs.cost_actual_usd` claimed to be the bill and was only the model half: `CostTally` was token-shaped, so a Hunt fetching through hostile domains under-reported itself and the Bright Data free plan's **5,000 monthly credits** drained with no counter anywhere — exhausting it would have dropped tier 3 off the ladder mid-run as an unexplained failure. The tally moves out of `llm/` into `worker/costs.py` so `fetching/tier3.py` can bill without importing the model seam, keeping the two channels separable (a blended number cannot answer *how much of this was the model?*). Billing happens on **response received**, not on success: the provider ran and charged for a request the target blocked, and over-counting a provider-side rejection is the safe direction for a budget meter while under-counting is not; a transport failure that never landed is never billed. An unpriced provider records calls at $0 rather than a guess. Migration `20260824000000_job_stage_costs.sql` records spend per `(job, stage)` at `numeric(12,6)` — four decimals truncates a single $0.0015 request toward zero — replaced rather than accumulated on re-run, with `tier3_credit_usage` counting per provider per calendar month. Per stage rather than per call because per-call telemetry is Langfuse's job. Also fixed while in the seam: each retry opened a fresh tally and only the winning attempt counted, so a stage that burned three credits before succeeding reported one; spend now banks in a `finally` covering success, retry, fatal and raised checkpoints alike. **Verification:** 12 new worker tests; worker **699 passed**, shared **46**, api **251**; advisors show no RLS or security-definer findings for the new objects |

A door's identity across Visits is its **human-typed label**, which is forced rather than chosen: there is no unit inventory to key on, and inventing one to make the roll-up tidier would undo §18's deferral this feature was built around. Two details fell out of reading the existing code rather than guessing: `ValueState` has **no `confirmed`** — the vocabulary is extracted/manual/estimated/unknown, and `manual` is not a compromise but the honest answer, since a person confirmed the figure and a person accepted it; and §9.6 already scopes a quoted base rent to **the Floor Plan you were standing in**, so accepting scopes to the walked unit's plan and falls back to Property only for a described unit that matched none. Three defences the database owns rather than the API: the offered Listing must be **the same Property that was toured** (offering one building's rent to another is the loud failure), one **live offer per destination** so the drawer never asks the same question twice with different numbers, and a decision is a pair — status, decider and time move together or the row is lying. | 2.0.99 | 2026-07-30 | **VC-6 landed — local-first answers, the sync badge, and the conflict picker (DESIGN §9.7, §20 v3.33).** Migration `20260821000000_visit_entry_conflicts.sql`. The slice's real content is two decisions. **First, what a conflict is.** The view defines an open fork as rows sharing a `prev_entry_id` *whose answers differ*, surfacing one option per distinct answer, open until some row descends from a branch — which lets the picker resolve it and also lets an ordinary later answer settle it, since nagging about a value its own author already replaced is noise. Requiring disagreement is load-bearing: two members reaching the same answer have nothing to decide, and it makes the view immune to duplicate replay. **That replay is real and I reproduced it** — a queued write that flushes just as the tab navigates can be restored from storage and sent twice, which I saw as two identical rows in `visit_entries`. With a null parent it was harmless; with a parent it would have forked against itself and shown a phantom conflict. Byte-identical branches are agreement whatever produced them, so it now costs one redundant history row. **A true idempotency key (a client-generated id, uniquely indexed) is the stronger fix and is not done — recorded as follow-up**, since it changes the API contract; the user-visible defect is closed. **Second, what survives a reload.** The mutation function and its invalidation live on the query client (`lib/offline.ts`), not in the hook, because a restored write is rebuilt in a page where the hook's closure is gone — so `visitId` travels as a variable rather than being captured, and the persister is narrowed to visit-scoped keys so a cold start cannot resurrect stale listings or jobs. Optimistic rows are marked `optimistic:` and may never be claimed as a `prev_entry_id`: pointing at a row the server has never seen would fail the foreign key or manufacture a fork against nothing. **Three things worth keeping for next time.** (a) `PersistQueryClientProvider` restores the queue but does **not** resume it — without `onSuccess={() => queryClient.resumePausedMutations()}` an answer taken offline survived the reload and then sat there forever, which looks exactly like data loss; found only by driving it. (b) A literal *reload while offline* is untestable in dev: with no service worker the dev server's own assets are unreachable and WebKit errors out, so the honest test is that the queue persists and flushes on restore. (c) The picker rendered a raw lowercase `ok` beside a title-cased `Problem` — a Check *stores* `ok`/`problem` but *reads* Fine/Problem on the control, and a picker showing the stored token asks the reader to choose between an answer and a synonym for it. Caught in a 390px screenshot, not by a test. **Verification:** clean `supabase db reset`; api **221 passed** (7 new, pinning the view's semantics against a real database); frontend **85 files / 618 tests** (26 new); tsc, eslint, `vite build`, ruff clean; end-to-end in WebKit including a two-member offline fork resolved through the UI. |
| 2.0.98 | 2026-07-30 | **VC-5 landed — Realtime table wiring, per-Visit Presence, bylines, team aggregates (DESIGN §13.3, §9.7).** Five visit tables added to `realtime.ts`; `visit_entries` and `visit_defects` invalidate on their own keys so a write by one member lands in everyone's checklist without a reload. Bylines read `author_user_id` and are shown **only on shared answers** — attribution on a per-member item would be noise, since the owner is the author by construction. **Presence is the part that needed real debugging, and the lesson is worth keeping.** It is one channel per Visit, ref-counted across consumers, with teardown deferred ~250ms so a StrictMode remount or a fast back-navigation reuses the channel instead of racing it; the naive "remove the stale channel, open a fresh one" remount handling opens a *second* channel tracking under the same presence key while the first is still joined, and no single teardown closes both. The load-bearing discovery: **`track()` appends a presence meta and `untrack()` removes only what one call left**, so the perfectly ordinary sequence of tracking at join and again once the section is known strands a meta that nothing can ever reach — the member then shows as present **forever** after they leave. Section changes therefore untrack-then-track, serialised through a promise chain; the sub-second blink in other people's avatar rows is the correct trade, because presence is ambient and a permanently wrong roster is not. Two testing traps recorded: `page.goto()` is a **hard** navigation, so React cleanup never runs and no untrack is sent — a leave test that navigates that way measures the server's heartbeat timeout, not the app, and will "fail" against correct code; and `getByText` cannot see a React-controlled `<textarea>`'s value, which lives in the `value` property and not the DOM text.
